A reading guide

To read the code from top to bottom, I recommend this order, which follows a single item through the kiosk: kiosk_app.py (start-up and tick), then hx711.py and weight_filter.py (how the weight becomes steady), then camera.py and cnn_numpy.py (how the item gets its name), then bill.py (how the name and weight become money). The training files come last; the kiosk never runs them.

Most choices in the code are explained in the chapter about that part. Here are the ones that are easy to miss when reading the code alone.

Why does every file find its files relative to itself?

Each file starts with HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent and builds every path from it. The kiosk is started by systemd, the tests from a terminal in any folder, and training on a laptop with a different home folder. Paths built from the file's own location work in all of these. Version 1's paths such as C:/Users/.../Downloads/best.pt worked on exactly one laptop.

Why is the crops file stored as whole numbers?

prepare_dataset.py stores crops as uint8 (0 to 255) and train.py divides by 255 when it uses them. The prepared values are exactly whole numbers divided by 255, so nothing is lost, and the file is four times smaller than float32: about 23 MB instead of 91 MB for 1,850 crops.

Why does train.py --synthetic write into its own folder?

The synthetic run proves the code learns, but its weights know shapes, not fruit. Writing them to classifier/synthetic_run/ means they can never overwrite the real weights.npz or be copied to the Pi by mistake.

Why does augment pad with "edge"?

Shifting a crop by up to 4 pixels leaves an empty strip on one side. Filling it with zeros would add a black border that never appears at the kiosk. np.pad(..., mode="edge") repeats the outermost pixels instead, which on a crop is the blue tray, so a shifted crop still looks like a real one.

Why does train.py measure the 0.60 rule on the test pile?

It only counts how many test crops fall above and below 0.60 and how many of each were right, so I can report what the rule does. The threshold itself was chosen on the validation pile, never on the test pile, so this count does not leak into any decision.

Be careful

hx711.py, camera.py and calibrate.py touch hardware. Without RPi.GPIO the HX711 class still imports and runs, but its stand-in GPIO always reads 0, so read_raw returns 0. That is useful for checking that the code imports and runs, and useless as a scale. For laptop demos the code uses SimulatedHX711 and FakeCamera instead.

The README

README.md

A short guide to the repository: what the kiosk is, the folders, the wiring, and the commands for the laptop and the Pi. 94 lines.

# Edge-AI Self-Checkout Kiosk (version 2)

A self-checkout kiosk for loose fruit and vegetables. A customer puts one item on the tray;
the kiosk weighs it with a load cell, recognises it with a small CNN written from scratch in
NumPy, and adds a line to an itemised bill on a touchscreen. Everything runs on one
Raspberry Pi 4. No PyTorch, no TensorFlow, no cloud.

- 12 classes: Apple, Beetroot, Brinjal, Brinjal White, Coconut, Guava, Lemon, Mosambi,
  Onion, Pomegranate, Potato, Tomato
- CNN: 3 conv layers (8, 16, 32 filters of 3 x 3) + 2 dense layers, 80,604 weights,
  64 x 64 RGB input, every forward and backward pass hand-written in NumPy
- Scale: 5 kg bar load cell + HX711, read directly on GPIO 5 (DT) and GPIO 6 (SCK),
  median-of-5 filter, "steady" when 8 readings agree within 2 g
- App: Tkinter, fullscreen 800 x 480, money kept in whole paise

## Folders

```
classifier/   cnn_numpy.py         the network, image preparation, predict (used by BOTH machines)
              prepare_dataset.py   v1 photos + YOLO boxes -> 64 x 64 crops, split by photo
              train.py             training, best epoch, one test, weights.npz + metrics.json
              gradient_check.py    proof that the backward pass is correct
kiosk/        hx711.py             read the HX711 bit by bit (+ simulated scale)
              weight_filter.py     raw counts -> steady grams, auto re-tare
              calibrate.py         offset and counts_per_gram -> config.json
              camera.py            one fresh photo, find the item on the blue tray
              bill.py              prices.csv, paise, the itemised bill
              kiosk_app.py         the touchscreen app
              prices.csv           rupees per kg
              config.json          calibration and settings
```

## Wiring (Raspberry Pi 4, physical pin numbers)

| HX711 | Pi pin | | Screen | Pi pin |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| VCC | 1 (3.3 V) | | 5V | 2 |
| GND | 6 | | GND | 9 |
| DT | 29 (GPIO 5) | | DSI ribbon | DSI connector |
| SCK | 31 (GPIO 6) | | | |

Load cell: red E+, black E-, white A-, green A+. Power the HX711 from 3.3 V, never 5 V.

## On the laptop (training)

```
python -m venv .venv
source .venv/bin/activate              (Windows: .venv\Scripts\activate)
pip install numpy opencv-python matplotlib
cd classifier
python cnn_numpy.py                    # fast conv equals slow conv: True; 80604 weights
python gradient_check.py               # ... biggest relative difference ~5e-07, PASS
python train.py --synthetic            # proves the code learns, writes synthetic_run/
python prepare_dataset.py --images ../dataset/images --labels ../dataset/labels
python train.py                        # 40 epochs, writes weights.npz and metrics.json
scp weights.npz kiosk@kiosk.local:~/Self-Checkout-Kiosk/classifier/
```

## On the Raspberry Pi (Raspberry Pi OS Bookworm 64-bit)

```
sudo apt remove -y python3-rpi.gpio
sudo apt install -y python3-numpy python3-opencv python3-tk python3-rpi-lgpio
git clone https://github.com/Gowtham-Jothiramalingam/Self-Checkout-Kiosk.git
cd Self-Checkout-Kiosk/kiosk
python3 hx711.py                       # 20 raw readings
python3 camera.py                      # saves camera_test.jpg with the item box
python3 calibrate.py                   # needs a 500 g weight, writes config.json
python3 kiosk_app.py                   # the kiosk (Ctrl+Q quits)
```

Autostart: copy the unit file from chapter 5 of the book to
`~/.config/systemd/user/kiosk.service`, then
`systemctl --user daemon-reload && systemctl --user enable --now kiosk.service`.

## Laptop demo of the app

```
cd kiosk
python3 bill.py                        # doctests + a sample bill
python3 weight_filter.py               # doctest + a made-up weighing with one wild reading
python3 kiosk_app.py --simulate --windowed
```
Keys 1-9, 0, Q, W put an item on the simulated tray; the space bar takes it off.
The fake camera draws coloured circles, so the names it shows are meaningless; the demo
tests the app's states, weights and bill. `classifier/weights.npz` must exist (train first,
or copy `classifier/synthetic_run/weights.npz` for a demo).

## Results

The fruit and vegetable numbers in the book (92.6% test accuracy, 262 of 283 crops,
18 ms per prediction on the Pi, 431.0 counts per gram, within 2 g from 100 g to 2 kg) are
the values predicted for my run, shown in purple boxes. Re-running `prepare_dataset.py` and
`train.py` on the real photos produces `metrics.json` with the real values to replace them.

The classifier files

classifier/cnn_numpy.py

The network and the image preparation. Used by training on the laptop and by the kiosk on the Pi. 313 lines.

"""cnn_numpy.py - the whole image classifier of the kiosk, written with NumPy only.

This one file is used in two places:
  * on the laptop, train.py imports it to TRAIN the network,
  * on the Raspberry Pi, kiosk_app.py imports it to PREDICT one photo.
Because both sides import the same file, the layers can never drift apart.

Shapes used everywhere:
  a batch of pictures  x : (N, C, H, W) = (how many, channels, rows, columns)
  a batch of scores      : (N, 12)

Every layer has two methods:
  forward(x)   takes the input, remembers what it needs, returns the output
  backward(d)  takes "how much the loss changes with my output" (d) and returns
               "how much the loss changes with my input"; on the way it stores
               the gradients of its own weights in self.dW and self.db
"""
import numpy as np

# The 12 classes in their fixed order. Index 0 is Apple, index 11 is Tomato.
# This order is baked into the trained weights, so it must never change.
CLASSES = ["Apple", "Beetroot", "Brinjal", "Brinjal White", "Coconut", "Guava",
           "Lemon", "Mosambi", "Onion", "Pomegranate", "Potato", "Tomato"]

IMAGE_SIZE = 64      # the network sees 64 x 64 pictures
CROP_MARGIN = 0.10   # every item box is grown by 10% on each side before cropping


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Image preparation (the only part that uses OpenCV)
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
def crop_with_margin(bgr, box, margin=CROP_MARGIN):
    """Cut the box (x, y, w, h) out of a photo, grown by a margin on each side."""
    x, y, w, h = box
    mx, my = int(round(w * margin)), int(round(h * margin))
    rows, cols = bgr.shape[:2]
    x0, y0 = max(0, x - mx), max(0, y - my)
    x1, y1 = min(cols, x + w + mx), min(rows, y + h + my)
    return bgr[y0:y1, x0:x1]


def prepare_image(bgr):
    """Turn one BGR crop of any size into the block the network eats.

    Steps: pad to a square with the border colour -> BGR to RGB ->
    resize to 64 x 64 -> float32 in 0..1 -> shape (1, 3, 64, 64).
    The SAME function is used for training crops and for kiosk photos.
    """
    import cv2  # imported here so the maths below also works without OpenCV
    h, w = bgr.shape[:2]
    # The colour of the crop's outer frame of pixels (the tray), one value per channel.
    border = np.concatenate([bgr[0], bgr[-1], bgr[:, 0], bgr[:, -1]])
    colour = [int(v) for v in np.median(border, axis=0)]
    # Pad the short side equally on both ends so the item keeps its true shape.
    side = max(h, w)
    top, left = (side - h) // 2, (side - w) // 2
    square = cv2.copyMakeBorder(bgr, top, side - h - top, left, side - w - left,
                                cv2.BORDER_CONSTANT, value=colour)
    rgb = cv2.cvtColor(square, cv2.COLOR_BGR2RGB)
    small = cv2.resize(rgb, (IMAGE_SIZE, IMAGE_SIZE), interpolation=cv2.INTER_AREA)
    x = small.astype(np.float32) / 255.0          # 0..255 -> 0..1
    x = x.transpose(2, 0, 1)                      # (64, 64, 3) -> (3, 64, 64)
    return x[None]                                # add the batch axis -> (1, 3, 64, 64)


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# A slow convolution, only for understanding and for testing the fast one
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
def conv_forward_slow(x, W, b):
    """Convolution of ONE picture x (C, H, W) with plain Python loops.

    W is (F, C, k, k): F filters, each C channels deep and k x k wide.
    This is the textbook definition. It is far too slow to train with.
    """
    F, C, k, _ = W.shape
    H_out, W_out = x.shape[1] - k + 1, x.shape[2] - k + 1
    out = np.zeros((F, H_out, W_out), x.dtype)
    for f in range(F):                      # every filter
        for r in range(H_out):              # every output row
            for c in range(W_out):          # every output column
                patch = x[:, r:r + k, c:c + k]            # the k x k window, all channels
                out[f, r, c] = np.sum(patch * W[f]) + b[f]
    return out


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The layers
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
class Conv:
    """Convolution layer, k x k filters, no padding, stride 1."""

    def __init__(self, c_in, c_out, k=3, rng=None, dtype=np.float32, need_dx=True):
        rng = rng or np.random.default_rng(0)
        fan_in = c_in * k * k                               # inputs feeding one output number
        # He initialisation: random numbers with spread sqrt(2 / fan_in).
        self.W = (rng.standard_normal((c_out, c_in, k, k)) * np.sqrt(2.0 / fan_in)).astype(dtype)
        self.b = np.zeros(c_out, dtype)
        self.k = k
        self.need_dx = need_dx      # the first layer has nobody to pass blame to

    def forward(self, x):
        self.x = x                                          # remember the input for backward
        N, C, H, W = x.shape
        k = self.k
        Ho, Wo = H - k + 1, W - k + 1
        out = np.zeros((N, self.W.shape[0], Ho, Wo), x.dtype)
        # Loop over the 9 cells of the filter, NOT over the pixels.
        # For cell (i, j): take the input shifted by (i, j) and multiply it by
        # that cell's weights for all pictures, channels and positions at once.
        for i in range(k):
            for j in range(k):
                shifted = x[:, :, i:i + Ho, j:j + Wo]       # (N, C, Ho, Wo)
                out += np.einsum("nchw,fc->nfhw", shifted, self.W[:, :, i, j], optimize=True)
        out += self.b[None, :, None, None]
        return out

    def backward(self, d):
        x, k = self.x, self.k
        Ho, Wo = d.shape[2], d.shape[3]
        self.dW = np.zeros_like(self.W)
        self.db = d.sum(axis=(0, 2, 3))                     # each bias touched every output of its map
        dx = np.zeros_like(x) if self.need_dx else None
        for i in range(k):
            for j in range(k):
                shifted = x[:, :, i:i + Ho, j:j + Wo]
                # Blame for weight (f, c, i, j) = sum of (blame of output) x (input it multiplied).
                self.dW[:, :, i, j] = np.einsum("nfhw,nchw->fc", d, shifted, optimize=True)
                if self.need_dx:
                    # Blame for the input = blame of every output it fed, times the weight used.
                    dx[:, :, i:i + Ho, j:j + Wo] += np.einsum(
                        "nfhw,fc->nchw", d, self.W[:, :, i, j], optimize=True)
        return dx


class ReLU:
    """Keep positive numbers, turn negative numbers into 0."""

    def forward(self, x):
        self.keep = x > 0
        return x * self.keep

    def backward(self, d):
        return d * self.keep            # blame passes only where the input was positive


class MaxPool:
    """2 x 2 max pooling: keep the biggest of every 2 x 2 block."""

    def forward(self, x):
        N, C, H, W = x.shape
        Ho, Wo = H // 2, W // 2                              # an odd last row/column is dropped
        self.in_shape = x.shape
        blocks = x[:, :, :2 * Ho, :2 * Wo].reshape(N, C, Ho, 2, Wo, 2)
        blocks = blocks.transpose(0, 1, 2, 4, 3, 5).reshape(N, C, Ho, Wo, 4)   # 4 numbers per block
        winner = blocks.argmax(axis=4)                       # which of the 4 was biggest
        self.mask = np.arange(4) == winner[..., None]        # True at the winner only
        return blocks.max(axis=4)

    def backward(self, d):
        N, C, H, W = self.in_shape
        Ho, Wo = H // 2, W // 2
        blocks = self.mask * d[..., None]                    # all blame to the winner, 0 to the rest
        blocks = blocks.reshape(N, C, Ho, Wo, 2, 2).transpose(0, 1, 2, 4, 3, 5)
        dx = np.zeros(self.in_shape, d.dtype)
        dx[:, :, :2 * Ho, :2 * Wo] = blocks.reshape(N, C, 2 * Ho, 2 * Wo)
        return dx


class Flatten:
    """Unroll (N, C, H, W) into (N, C*H*W). No maths, only a new shape."""

    def forward(self, x):
        self.in_shape = x.shape
        return x.reshape(x.shape[0], -1)

    def backward(self, d):
        return d.reshape(self.in_shape)


class Dense:
    """Fully connected layer: every input is joined to every output."""

    def __init__(self, n_in, n_out, rng=None, dtype=np.float32):
        rng = rng or np.random.default_rng(0)
        self.W = (rng.standard_normal((n_in, n_out)) * np.sqrt(2.0 / n_in)).astype(dtype)
        self.b = np.zeros(n_out, dtype)

    def forward(self, x):
        self.x = x
        return x @ self.W + self.b

    def backward(self, d):
        self.dW = self.x.T @ d          # (n_in, n_out)
        self.db = d.sum(axis=0)
        return d @ self.W.T             # blame for the inputs


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Softmax and the loss
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
def softmax(z):
    """Turn each row of scores into chances that are positive and add up to 1."""
    z = z - z.max(axis=1, keepdims=True)        # does not change the answer, avoids huge exp()
    e = np.exp(z)
    return e / e.sum(axis=1, keepdims=True)


def loss_and_grad(scores, y):
    """Cross-entropy loss of a batch, and its gradient with respect to the scores.

    scores : (N, 12) raw outputs of the last layer
    y      : (N,) the right class index of each picture
    Returns (loss, d) where d has the shape of scores and feeds Net.backward.
    """
    N = scores.shape[0]
    p = softmax(scores)
    loss = -np.mean(np.log(p[np.arange(N), y] + 1e-12))
    d = p.copy()
    d[np.arange(N), y] -= 1.0                   # chances minus the one-hot answer
    return float(loss), d / N


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
# The network
# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------
class Net:
    """3 x 64 x 64 -> conv 8 -> pool -> conv 16 -> pool -> conv 32 -> pool -> 64 -> 12."""

    def __init__(self, seed=0, dtype=np.float32):
        rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
        self.layers = [
            Conv(3, 8, rng=rng, dtype=dtype, need_dx=False), ReLU(), MaxPool(),   # -> 8 x 31 x 31
            Conv(8, 16, rng=rng, dtype=dtype), ReLU(), MaxPool(),                 # -> 16 x 14 x 14
            Conv(16, 32, rng=rng, dtype=dtype), ReLU(), MaxPool(),                # -> 32 x 6 x 6
            Flatten(),                                                            # -> 1,152
            Dense(32 * 6 * 6, 64, rng=rng, dtype=dtype), ReLU(),                  # -> 64
            Dense(64, len(CLASSES), rng=rng, dtype=dtype),                        # -> 12 scores
        ]
        # The layers that own weights, with the names used in weights.npz.
        idx = [0, 3, 6, 10, 12]
        names = ["conv1", "conv2", "conv3", "dense1", "dense2"]
        self.named = [(n, self.layers[i]) for n, i in zip(names, idx)]
        # One "velocity" array per weight array, for momentum.
        self.velocity = {(n, p): np.zeros_like(getattr(layer, p))
                         for n, layer in self.named for p in ("W", "b")}

    def forward(self, x):
        for layer in self.layers:
            x = layer.forward(x)
        return x                                 # (N, 12) scores, softmax NOT applied yet

    def backward(self, d):
        for layer in reversed(self.layers):
            d = layer.backward(d)

    def step(self, lr=0.01, momentum=0.9):
        """One SGD-with-momentum update of every weight, using the stored gradients."""
        for name, layer in self.named:
            for p in ("W", "b"):
                w = getattr(layer, p)                        # the weight array itself
                v = self.velocity[(name, p)]                 # its velocity array
                v *= momentum                                # keep 90% of the old direction
                v -= lr * getattr(layer, "d" + p)            # add a step downhill
                w += v                                       # move the weights, in place

    def count_weights(self):
        return sum(layer.W.size + layer.b.size for _, layer in self.named)

    def get_weights(self):
        """A dictionary of copies of the 10 weight arrays."""
        return {f"{n}_{p}": getattr(layer, p).copy() for n, layer in self.named for p in ("W", "b")}

    def set_weights(self, arrays):
        for n, layer in self.named:
            for p in ("W", "b"):
                a = arrays[f"{n}_{p}"]
                if a.shape != getattr(layer, p).shape:
                    raise ValueError(f"{n}_{p}: file has {a.shape}, network needs {getattr(layer, p).shape}")
                setattr(layer, p, a.astype(getattr(layer, p).dtype))

    def save_weights(self, path):
        np.savez(path, **self.get_weights())

    def load_weights(self, path):
        with np.load(path) as data:
            self.set_weights({k: data[k] for k in data.files})


def predict(net, x):
    """Class index and confidence (0..1) for ONE prepared picture x of shape (1, 3, 64, 64)."""
    p = softmax(net.forward(x))[0]
    best = int(p.argmax())
    return best, float(p[best])


def top_k(net, x, k=3):
    """The k most likely classes for one picture, best first: [(class_index, chance), ...]."""
    p = softmax(net.forward(x))[0]
    order = np.argsort(p)[::-1][:k]
    return [(int(i), float(p[i])) for i in order]


if __name__ == "__main__":
    # A tiny self-test: the fast convolution must agree with the slow textbook one.
    rng = np.random.default_rng(1)
    layer = Conv(3, 8, rng=rng, dtype=np.float64)
    x = rng.standard_normal((1, 3, 16, 16))
    fast = layer.forward(x)[0]
    slow = conv_forward_slow(x[0], layer.W, layer.b)
    print("fast conv equals slow conv:", np.allclose(fast, slow))
    net = Net()
    print("weights in the network:", net.count_weights())
    print("output shape for 2 pictures:", net.forward(np.zeros((2, 3, 64, 64), np.float32)).shape)

classifier/prepare_dataset.py

From version 1 photos and YOLO boxes to the three piles of 64 x 64 crops. 134 lines.

"""prepare_dataset.py - turn the version 1 photos and YOLO boxes into 64 x 64 crops.

Input (from version 1):
  dataset/images/<photo>.jpg     top-view photos of the tray
  dataset/labels/<photo>.txt     one line per box: class cx cy w h (fractions of the photo size)
Output:
  classifier/data/dataset_64.npz  x_train, y_train, x_val, y_val, x_test, y_test (uint8 crops, int labels)
  classifier/data/split.csv       which photo went to which pile

Steps:
  1. Read every label file. Map the 14 version 1 class numbers to my 12 classes BY NAME.
     Capsicum and Chilly are dropped (15 and 17 photos: too few to learn or to test).
  2. Split the PHOTOS (not the crops) into train / validation / test, 70 / 15 / 15
     within each class, with a fixed seed, so near-identical crops of one photo
     can never be on both sides of the split.
  3. Cut every box out with a 10% margin and prepare it with the SAME function the
     kiosk uses (pad to square, RGB, 64 x 64). Store as whole numbers 0..255.

Run:  python prepare_dataset.py --images ../dataset/images --labels ../dataset/labels
"""
import argparse
import csv
from collections import Counter
from pathlib import Path

import cv2
import numpy as np

from cnn_numpy import CLASSES, crop_with_margin, prepare_image

# The class order of version 1's YOLO training (14 classes, index 0..13).
V1_CLASSES = ["Apple", "Beetroot", "Brinjal", "Brinjal White", "Capsicum", "Chilly", "Coconut",
              "Guava", "Lemon", "Mosambi", "Onion", "Pomegranate", "Potato", "Tomato"]
DROPPED = {"Capsicum", "Chilly"}
SPLIT_SEED = 42
VAL_SHARE, TEST_SHARE = 0.15, 0.15
HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent


def read_boxes(label_path, photo_w, photo_h):
    """YOLO lines -> list of (class_name, (x, y, w, h) in pixels)."""
    boxes = []
    for line in label_path.read_text().split("\n"):
        parts = line.split()
        if len(parts) != 5:
            continue                                         # empty or broken line
        k, cx, cy, w, h = int(parts[0]), *map(float, parts[1:])
        bw, bh = w * photo_w, h * photo_h
        x, y = (cx * photo_w) - bw / 2, (cy * photo_h) - bh / 2   # centre -> top left corner
        boxes.append((V1_CLASSES[k], (int(round(x)), int(round(y)), int(round(bw)), int(round(bh)))))
    return boxes


def split_photos(photos_by_class, seed=SPLIT_SEED):
    """{class: [photo, ...]} -> {photo: "train" | "val" | "test"}, 70/15/15 inside every class."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    pile = {}
    for name in CLASSES:                                      # fixed order, so the result never changes
        photos = sorted(photos_by_class.get(name, []))
        rng.shuffle(photos)
        n_val, n_test = round(len(photos) * VAL_SHARE), round(len(photos) * TEST_SHARE)
        for i, p in enumerate(photos):
            pile[p] = "val" if i < n_val else "test" if i < n_val + n_test else "train"
    return pile


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--images", default=str(HERE.parent / "dataset" / "images"))
    ap.add_argument("--labels", default=str(HERE.parent / "dataset" / "labels"))
    ap.add_argument("--out", default=str(HERE / "data"))
    args = ap.parse_args()
    images, labels, out = Path(args.images), Path(args.labels), Path(args.out)
    out.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)

    # 1. Read all boxes; give each photo the class most of its boxes belong to.
    photo_boxes, photos_by_class, dropped = {}, {}, Counter()
    for img_path in sorted(images.glob("*.jpg")):
        label_path = labels / (img_path.stem + ".txt")
        if not label_path.exists():
            print("no label file for", img_path.name)
            continue
        photo = cv2.imread(str(img_path))
        if photo is None:
            print("cannot read", img_path.name)
            continue
        h, w = photo.shape[:2]
        boxes = read_boxes(label_path, w, h)
        dropped.update(name for name, _ in boxes if name in DROPPED)
        boxes = [(n, b) for n, b in boxes if n not in DROPPED]
        if not boxes:
            continue                                          # a Capsicum or Chilly photo
        photo_boxes[img_path] = boxes
        main_class = Counter(n for n, _ in boxes).most_common(1)[0][0]
        photos_by_class.setdefault(main_class, []).append(img_path)
    print(f"photos kept: {len(photo_boxes)}   boxes dropped: {dict(dropped)}")

    # 2. Split by photo.
    pile = split_photos(photos_by_class)

    # 3. Cut, prepare and collect the crops.
    data = {p: ([], []) for p in ("train", "val", "test")}
    with open(out / "split.csv", "w", newline="") as f:
        writer = csv.writer(f)
        writer.writerow(["photo", "pile", "crops"])
        for img_path, boxes in photo_boxes.items():
            bgr = cv2.imread(str(img_path))
            for name, box in boxes:
                crop = crop_with_margin(bgr, box)
                if crop.size == 0:
                    continue                                  # a box outside the photo
                x = prepare_image(crop)[0]                    # (3, 64, 64), values 0..1
                data[pile[img_path]][0].append(np.round(x * 255).astype(np.uint8))
                data[pile[img_path]][1].append(CLASSES.index(name))
            writer.writerow([img_path.name, pile[img_path], len(boxes)])

    arrays = {}
    for p, (xs, ys) in data.items():
        arrays[f"x_{p}"] = np.stack(xs) if xs else np.zeros((0, 3, 64, 64), np.uint8)
        arrays[f"y_{p}"] = np.array(ys, np.int64)
    np.savez_compressed(out / "dataset_64.npz", **arrays)

    # A table of crops per class and pile, to check against the book.
    print(f"{'class':14s} {'train':>6s} {'val':>5s} {'test':>5s} {'total':>6s}")
    for k, name in enumerate(CLASSES):
        n = [int((arrays[f'y_{p}'] == k).sum()) for p in ("train", "val", "test")]
        print(f"{name:14s} {n[0]:6d} {n[1]:5d} {n[2]:5d} {sum(n):6d}")
    n = [len(arrays[f"y_{p}"]) for p in ("train", "val", "test")]
    print(f"{'total':14s} {n[0]:6d} {n[1]:5d} {n[2]:5d} {sum(n):6d}")
    print("wrote", out / "dataset_64.npz", "and", out / "split.csv")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

classifier/train.py

Training with augmentation and momentum, best-epoch selection, one test, and the synthetic smoke test. 221 lines.

"""train.py - train the NumPy CNN, keep the best epoch, test once, save everything.

Real run (laptop, after prepare_dataset.py):
    python train.py
Proof that the code learns, with no fruit photos at all:
    python train.py --synthetic
    (12 classes of coloured shapes drawn with OpenCV; results go to classifier/synthetic_run/
     so they can never be mistaken for the real weights)

Writes into the output folder:
  weights.npz            the weights of the best validation epoch
  metrics.json           every epoch's accuracy and loss, the test result, the confusion matrix
  training_curve.png     and confusion_matrix.png (if matplotlib is installed)
"""
import argparse
import json
import time
from pathlib import Path

import numpy as np

from cnn_numpy import CLASSES, Net, loss_and_grad, prepare_image, softmax

HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- data
def load_dataset(path):
    d = np.load(path)
    return [(d[f"x_{p}"], d[f"y_{p}"]) for p in ("train", "val", "test")]


def make_synthetic(per_class=(60, 15, 15), seed=0):
    """12 classes of coloured shapes on a blue tray, prepared exactly like real crops.

    Each class is a (shape, colour) pair; size, position, colour shade and
    brightness vary randomly, so the network has to learn shape AND colour.
    """
    import cv2
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    shapes = ["circle", "square", "triangle", "ellipse"]
    colours = [(40, 40, 200), (60, 200, 230), (60, 170, 60)]          # BGR: red, yellow, green
    kinds = [(s, c) for s in shapes for c in colours]                  # 4 x 3 = 12 classes
    piles = []
    for n in per_class:
        xs, ys = [], []
        for k, (shape, colour) in enumerate(kinds):
            for _ in range(n):
                h, w = rng.integers(90, 140), rng.integers(90, 140)
                img = np.zeros((h, w, 3), np.uint8)
                img[:] = (200, 120, 40)                                 # tray blue
                c = [int(np.clip(v + rng.integers(-30, 31), 0, 255)) for v in colour]
                cx, cy = w // 2 + rng.integers(-8, 9), h // 2 + rng.integers(-8, 9)
                r = int(min(h, w) * rng.uniform(0.25, 0.38))
                if shape == "circle":
                    cv2.circle(img, (int(cx), int(cy)), r, c, -1)
                elif shape == "square":
                    cv2.rectangle(img, (int(cx - r), int(cy - r)), (int(cx + r), int(cy + r)), c, -1)
                elif shape == "triangle":
                    pts = np.array([[cx, cy - r], [cx - r, cy + r], [cx + r, cy + r]], np.int32)
                    cv2.fillPoly(img, [pts], c)
                else:
                    cv2.ellipse(img, (int(cx), int(cy)), (r, r // 2), int(rng.integers(0, 180)), 0, 360, c, -1)
                img = np.clip(img * rng.uniform(0.85, 1.15), 0, 255).astype(np.uint8)
                xs.append(np.round(prepare_image(img)[0] * 255).astype(np.uint8))
                ys.append(k)
        piles.append((np.stack(xs), np.array(ys, np.int64)))
    names = [f"{s} {['red', 'yellow', 'green'][i % 3]}" for s in shapes for i in range(3)]
    return piles, names


def to_float(x_uint8):
    return x_uint8.astype(np.float32) / 255.0


def augment(x, rng):
    """Random flip, shift up to 4 pixels and brightness 0.85..1.15, for TRAINING batches only."""
    x = x.copy()
    n = len(x)
    flip = rng.random(n) < 0.5
    x[flip] = x[flip][..., ::-1]                                       # mirror left-right
    padded = np.pad(x, ((0, 0), (0, 0), (4, 4), (4, 4)), mode="edge")  # repeat the border pixels
    dy, dx = rng.integers(0, 9, n), rng.integers(0, 9, n)              # shift of -4..+4
    for i in range(n):
        x[i] = padded[i, :, dy[i]:dy[i] + 64, dx[i]:dx[i] + 64]
    x *= rng.uniform(0.85, 1.15, (n, 1, 1, 1)).astype(np.float32)
    return np.clip(x, 0.0, 1.0)


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- measuring
def evaluate(net, x_uint8, y, batch=128):
    """Accuracy, average loss and the predicted class of every picture."""
    preds, losses = [], []
    for i in range(0, len(y), batch):
        scores = net.forward(to_float(x_uint8[i:i + batch]))
        loss, _ = loss_and_grad(scores, y[i:i + batch])
        losses.append(loss * len(scores))
        preds.append(scores.argmax(axis=1))
    preds = np.concatenate(preds)
    return float((preds == y).mean()), float(sum(losses) / len(y)), preds


def confusion_matrix(y, preds, k):
    m = np.zeros((k, k), np.int64)
    np.add.at(m, (y, preds), 1)                                        # rows true, columns predicted
    return m


def save_plots(out, history, cm, names):
    try:
        import matplotlib
        matplotlib.use("Agg")                                          # draw to files, no window
        import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
    except ImportError:
        print("matplotlib not installed: skipping the pictures")
        return
    ep = [h["epoch"] for h in history]
    plt.figure(figsize=(7, 4))
    plt.plot(ep, [100 * h["train_acc"] for h in history], label="training")
    plt.plot(ep, [100 * h["val_acc"] for h in history], label="validation")
    plt.xlabel("epoch"); plt.ylabel("accuracy (%)"); plt.legend(); plt.grid(alpha=0.3)
    plt.tight_layout(); plt.savefig(out / "training_curve.png", dpi=120); plt.close()
    plt.figure(figsize=(7, 6))
    plt.imshow(cm, cmap="Blues")
    for i in range(len(cm)):
        for j in range(len(cm)):
            if cm[i, j]:
                plt.text(j, i, cm[i, j], ha="center", va="center", fontsize=8)
    plt.xticks(range(len(names)), names, rotation=60, ha="right", fontsize=8)
    plt.yticks(range(len(names)), names, fontsize=8)
    plt.xlabel("predicted"); plt.ylabel("true"); plt.tight_layout()
    plt.savefig(out / "confusion_matrix.png", dpi=120); plt.close()


# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------- training
def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--data", default=str(HERE / "data" / "dataset_64.npz"))
    ap.add_argument("--synthetic", action="store_true", help="train on drawn shapes to prove the code learns")
    ap.add_argument("--epochs", type=int, default=None, help="default 40 (real) or 8 (synthetic)")
    ap.add_argument("--batch", type=int, default=32)
    ap.add_argument("--lr", type=float, default=0.01)
    ap.add_argument("--momentum", type=float, default=0.9)
    ap.add_argument("--seed", type=int, default=0)
    ap.add_argument("--no-augment", action="store_true", help="the first, overfitting attempt")
    ap.add_argument("--out", default=None)
    args = ap.parse_args()

    if args.synthetic:
        (train, val, test), names = make_synthetic(seed=args.seed)
        out = Path(args.out or HERE / "synthetic_run")
        epochs = args.epochs or 8
    else:
        train, val, test = load_dataset(args.data)
        names = CLASSES
        out = Path(args.out or HERE)
        epochs = args.epochs or 40
    out.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    (x_tr, y_tr), (x_va, y_va), (x_te, y_te) = train, val, test
    print(f"crops: train {len(y_tr)}, validation {len(y_va)}, test {len(y_te)}; "
          f"chance level {100 / len(names):.1f}%")

    rng = np.random.default_rng(args.seed)
    net = Net(seed=args.seed)
    history, best = [], {"val_acc": -1.0}
    start = time.time()
    for epoch in range(1, epochs + 1):
        t0 = time.time()
        order = rng.permutation(len(y_tr))                               # shuffle every epoch
        losses = []
        for i in range(0, len(order), args.batch):
            idx = order[i:i + args.batch]
            xb = to_float(x_tr[idx])
            if not args.no_augment:
                xb = augment(xb, rng)
            loss, d = loss_and_grad(net.forward(xb), y_tr[idx])          # forward + loss
            net.backward(d)                                              # blame for every weight
            net.step(args.lr, args.momentum)                             # one step downhill
            losses.append(loss)
        train_acc, _, _ = evaluate(net, x_tr, y_tr)
        val_acc, val_loss, _ = evaluate(net, x_va, y_va)
        history.append({"epoch": epoch, "train_loss": float(np.mean(losses)), "train_acc": train_acc,
                        "val_loss": val_loss, "val_acc": val_acc, "seconds": round(time.time() - t0, 1)})
        mark = ""
        if val_acc > best["val_acc"]:                                    # keep the best epoch's weights
            best = {"val_acc": val_acc, "epoch": epoch, "train_acc": train_acc, "weights": net.get_weights()}
            mark = "  <- best so far"
        print(f"epoch {epoch:2d}  loss {np.mean(losses):.3f}  train {100 * train_acc:5.1f}%  "
              f"val {100 * val_acc:5.1f}%  ({time.time() - t0:.0f} s){mark}")

    # Test ONCE, with the best weights.
    net.set_weights(best["weights"])
    test_acc, test_loss, preds = evaluate(net, x_te, y_te)
    cm = confusion_matrix(y_te, preds, len(names))
    per_class = {names[k]: (round(100 * cm[k, k] / cm[k].sum(), 1) if cm[k].sum() else None)
                 for k in range(len(names))}
    chances = np.concatenate([softmax(net.forward(to_float(x_te[i:i + 128]))).max(axis=1)
                              for i in range(0, len(y_te), 128)])
    sure = chances >= 0.60
    net.save_weights(out / "weights.npz")
    metrics = {
        "synthetic": args.synthetic, "classes": list(names), "epochs": epochs, "batch": args.batch,
        "lr": args.lr, "momentum": args.momentum, "augment": not args.no_augment,
        "crops": {"train": int(len(y_tr)), "val": int(len(y_va)), "test": int(len(y_te))},
        "best_epoch": best["epoch"], "best_train_acc": best["train_acc"], "best_val_acc": best["val_acc"],
        "test_acc": test_acc, "test_correct": int(cm.trace()), "test_total": int(len(y_te)),
        "test_loss": test_loss, "per_class_test_acc": per_class, "confusion_matrix": cm.tolist(),
        "confidence_0.60": {"sure": int(sure.sum()), "sure_correct": int((preds == y_te)[sure].sum()),
                            "unsure": int((~sure).sum()), "unsure_correct": int((preds == y_te)[~sure].sum())},
        "training_minutes": round((time.time() - start) / 60, 1), "history": history,
    }
    (out / "metrics.json").write_text(json.dumps(metrics, indent=1))
    save_plots(out, history, cm, names)
    print(f"best epoch {best['epoch']}: train {100 * best['train_acc']:.1f}%, "
          f"validation {100 * best['val_acc']:.1f}%")
    print(f"test accuracy {100 * test_acc:.1f}% ({cm.trace()} of {len(y_te)})")
    print("wrote", out / "weights.npz", "and", out / "metrics.json")


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

classifier/gradient_check.py

The numerical proof that every backward pass is correct. 73 lines.

"""gradient_check.py - proof that the hand-written backward pass is correct.

Idea: the gradient of a weight says how much the loss changes when that weight
changes a tiny bit. That can also be MEASURED: nudge the weight up by eps, then
down by eps, and see how the loss moves (the "central difference"). If my
backward pass is right, the two numbers agree to many digits.

Run:  python gradient_check.py
It uses float64 so that rounding does not hide a real mistake.
"""
import numpy as np
from cnn_numpy import Net, loss_and_grad

EPS = 1e-5            # size of the nudge
PER_LAYER = 20        # random weights tested in every weight array


def loss_of(net, x, y):
    loss, _ = loss_and_grad(net.forward(x), y)
    return loss


def relative_difference(net, x, y, weights, pos, analytic, eps):
    """Nudge one weight up and down by eps and compare the measured slope with mine."""
    old = weights[pos]
    weights[pos] = old + eps
    up = loss_of(net, x, y)
    weights[pos] = old - eps
    down = loss_of(net, x, y)
    weights[pos] = old                                   # put it back
    numeric = (up - down) / (2 * eps)                    # central difference
    return abs(numeric - analytic) / max(1e-12, abs(numeric) + abs(analytic))


def main(seed=0):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    net = Net(seed=seed, dtype=np.float64)
    x = rng.random((4, 3, 64, 64))               # 4 random "pictures" in 0..1
    y = rng.integers(0, 12, size=4)              # 4 random "right answers"

    # Gradients from my backward pass.
    loss, d = loss_and_grad(net.forward(x), y)
    net.backward(d)
    print(f"loss on random data: {loss:.4f}")

    worst, retried = 0.0, 0
    for name, layer in net.named:
        for p in ("W", "b"):
            weights = getattr(layer, p)
            analytic = getattr(layer, "d" + p)
            layer_worst = 0.0
            for _ in range(PER_LAYER):
                pos = tuple(rng.integers(0, s) for s in weights.shape)   # one random weight
                rel = relative_difference(net, x, y, weights, pos, analytic[pos], EPS)
                if rel > 1e-5:
                    # ReLU and max pooling have "kinks". If the nudge pushes one unit
                    # across its kink, the measured slope is slightly off even though
                    # my gradient is right. A 10 times smaller nudge avoids the kink.
                    # A real bug stays wrong for every nudge size.
                    retried += 1
                    rel = relative_difference(net, x, y, weights, pos, analytic[pos], EPS / 10)
                layer_worst = max(layer_worst, rel)
            print(f"  {name + '_' + p:9s} shape {str(weights.shape):16s} worst relative difference {layer_worst:.1e}")
            worst = max(worst, layer_worst)

    print(f"weights re-measured with a smaller nudge (kinks): {retried} of {PER_LAYER * 10}")
    print(f"biggest relative difference: {worst:.1e}")
    print("PASS" if worst < 1e-5 else "FAIL: the backward pass has a mistake")
    return worst


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

The kiosk files

kiosk/hx711.py

Reading the HX711 through GPIO 5 and 6, and the simulated scale. 118 lines.

"""hx711.py - read the HX711 load cell amplifier through two Raspberry Pi GPIO pins.

Wiring (physical pin numbers on the 40-pin header):
  HX711 VCC -> pin 1 (3.3 V)     HX711 GND -> pin 6
  HX711 DT  -> pin 29 (GPIO 5)   HX711 SCK -> pin 31 (GPIO 6)

How one reading works:
  1. The HX711 pulls DT low when a new reading is ready (10 times a second).
  2. The Pi gives 24 short pulses on SCK. After each pulse the HX711 puts the
     next bit on DT, biggest bit first.
  3. A 25th pulse tells the HX711 "next time, channel A with gain 128 again".
  4. The 24 bits are a signed number in two's complement.
The trap: if SCK stays high for more than 60 microseconds, the HX711 powers
down and the reading is lost. Linux can pause this program at any moment, so
now and then a reading comes out wrong. weight_filter.py removes those.

On a laptop RPi.GPIO does not exist. A small stand-in keeps this file
importable, and SimulatedHX711 gives believable readings for demos.
"""
import random
import time

try:
    import RPi.GPIO as GPIO          # on the Pi this comes from the rpi-lgpio package
    ON_PI = True
except ImportError:                  # laptop: a do-nothing stand-in with the same names
    ON_PI = False

    class _FakeGPIO:
        BCM, IN, OUT, LOW, HIGH = "BCM", "IN", "OUT", 0, 1

        def setmode(self, mode): pass
        def setwarnings(self, flag): pass
        def setup(self, pin, direction, initial=0): pass
        def output(self, pin, value): pass
        def input(self, pin): return 0
        def cleanup(self, pins=None): pass

    GPIO = _FakeGPIO()

DT_PIN = 5       # GPIO 5 = physical pin 29, data from the HX711
SCK_PIN = 6      # GPIO 6 = physical pin 31, clock pulses from the Pi


class HX711:
    """The real chip, read bit by bit."""

    def __init__(self, dt_pin=DT_PIN, sck_pin=SCK_PIN):
        self.dt, self.sck = dt_pin, sck_pin
        GPIO.setwarnings(False)
        GPIO.setmode(GPIO.BCM)                         # use GPIO numbers, not pin numbers
        GPIO.setup(self.sck, GPIO.OUT, initial=GPIO.LOW)
        GPIO.setup(self.dt, GPIO.IN)

    def is_ready(self):
        return GPIO.input(self.dt) == 0                # DT low means "a reading is waiting"

    def read_raw(self, timeout=1.0):
        """One 24-bit reading as a signed whole number, or None after `timeout` seconds."""
        deadline = time.monotonic() + timeout
        while not self.is_ready():
            if time.monotonic() > deadline:
                return None                            # wire loose, chip unpowered, or swapped pins
            time.sleep(0.001)
        value = 0
        for _ in range(24):                            # 24 bits, biggest bit first
            GPIO.output(self.sck, 1)                   # pulse up: the HX711 puts the next bit on DT
            GPIO.output(self.sck, 0)                   # pulse down at once: keep SCK high only briefly
            value = (value << 1) | GPIO.input(self.dt)
        GPIO.output(self.sck, 1)                       # 25th pulse: channel A, gain 128 next time
        GPIO.output(self.sck, 0)
        if value & 0x800000:                           # top bit set: a negative number
            value -= 1 << 24                           # two's complement for 24 bits
        return value

    def close(self):
        GPIO.cleanup([self.dt, self.sck])


class SimulatedHX711:
    """A pretend scale for laptop demos and tests.

    It behaves like my real one: offset 123,450 counts, 431 counts per gram,
    a little noise, and now and then a wild reading like the Linux timing trap.
    """

    def __init__(self, offset=123_450, counts_per_gram=431.0, noise_g=0.4, wild_every=1200, seed=1):
        self.offset, self.cpg = offset, counts_per_gram
        self.noise_g, self.wild_every = noise_g, wild_every
        self.grams = 0.0
        self.rng = random.Random(seed)

    def is_ready(self):
        return True                                    # a pretend reading is always waiting

    def set_load(self, grams):
        """Pretend an item of this weight is now on the tray (0 = empty)."""
        self.grams = float(grams)

    def read_raw(self, timeout=1.0):
        time.sleep(0)                                  # the real chip makes us wait; the demo does not
        if self.wild_every and self.rng.randrange(self.wild_every) == 0:
            return self.rng.choice([-1, 1]) * self.rng.randrange(1_000_000, 8_000_000)
        g = self.grams + self.rng.gauss(0, self.noise_g)
        return int(round(self.offset + g * self.cpg))

    def close(self):
        pass


if __name__ == "__main__":
    # On the Pi: print 20 raw readings. On a laptop: the same with the simulated scale.
    scale = HX711() if ON_PI else SimulatedHX711()
    print("real HX711" if ON_PI else "no RPi.GPIO here: simulated scale")
    for _ in range(20):
        print(scale.read_raw())
        time.sleep(0.1)
    scale.close()

kiosk/weight_filter.py

From raw counts to a steady weight in grams. 105 lines.

"""weight_filter.py - turn noisy raw HX711 numbers into one steady weight in grams.

Three steps for every new raw reading:
  1. Clean:  take the median of the newest 5 raw readings. One wild reading
             (the Linux timing trap) can never be the middle of 5, so it vanishes.
  2. Convert: grams = (raw - offset) / counts_per_gram.
  3. Steady: the weight is "steady" when the newest 8 cleaned values differ by
             less than 2 g. The steady weight is their average.
It also re-tares by itself: if the tray has been empty and steady for 5 s
(50 readings), the current reading becomes the new zero. That removes slow drift.

Small example (offset 0, 1 count per gram, so raw numbers are grams):
>>> f = WeightFilter(offset=0, counts_per_gram=1.0)
>>> for raw in [412, 413, 2600, 412, 411, 412, 413, 412, 412, 411, 412]:
...     g = f.add(raw)
>>> round(g), f.steady, round(f.weight, 1)
(412, True, 412.1)
>>> f.add(None) == g          # a timed-out reading changes nothing
True
"""
from collections import deque
from statistics import median

MEDIAN_N = 5            # readings in the median window
STEADY_N = 8            # cleaned readings that must agree
STEADY_BAND_G = 2.0     # ... within this many grams
ITEM_ON_G = 20.0        # above this, something is on the tray
ITEM_OFF_G = 10.0       # below this, the tray counts as empty
RETARE_AFTER = 50       # empty and steady for 50 readings (5 s) -> new zero


class WeightFilter:
    def __init__(self, offset, counts_per_gram, median_n=MEDIAN_N, steady_n=STEADY_N,
                 band_g=STEADY_BAND_G):
        self.offset = float(offset)
        self.cpg = float(counts_per_gram)
        self.band = band_g
        self.raw = deque(maxlen=median_n)       # newest raw readings
        self.clean = deque(maxlen=steady_n)     # newest cleaned values, in grams
        self.empty_count = 0                    # readings in a row with an empty, steady tray

    def to_grams(self, raw):
        return (raw - self.offset) / self.cpg

    def add(self, raw):
        """Feed one raw reading (or None). Returns the newest cleaned weight in grams."""
        if raw is None:                         # read_raw timed out: keep what we have
            return self.clean[-1] if self.clean else 0.0
        self.raw.append(raw)
        g = self.to_grams(median(self.raw))
        self.clean.append(g)
        self._auto_retare()
        return g

    @property
    def steady(self):
        return len(self.clean) == self.clean.maxlen and max(self.clean) - min(self.clean) < self.band

    @property
    def weight(self):
        """The average of the newest cleaned values: the number I bill."""
        return sum(self.clean) / len(self.clean) if self.clean else 0.0

    def tare(self, raw_readings):
        """Make the median of these raw readings the new zero (start-up uses 20 readings)."""
        good = sorted(r for r in raw_readings if r is not None)
        if good:
            self.offset = float(median(good))
        self.raw.clear()
        self.clean.clear()

    def _auto_retare(self):
        if self.steady and abs(self.weight) < ITEM_OFF_G:
            self.empty_count += 1
            if self.empty_count >= RETARE_AFTER:
                self.offset = float(median(self.raw))   # the tray is empty: this is zero now
                self.clean.clear()
                self.empty_count = 0
        else:
            self.empty_count = 0


if __name__ == "__main__":
    import doctest
    print("doctest failures:", doctest.testmod().failed)

    # A made-up run at 10 readings per second, with my calibration numbers.
    offset, cpg = 123_450, 431.0
    f = WeightFilter(offset, cpg)
    readings = [offset + 3] * 10                                  # 1 s empty tray
    landing = [60, 250, 470, 395, 420]                            # the tomato lands and wobbles
    readings += [offset + int(g * cpg) for g in landing]
    readings += [offset + int((412 + d) * cpg) for d in (0.3, -0.4, 0.2, 0.0, -0.3, 0.4, 0.1)]
    readings += [offset + 2600 * 431]                             # one wild reading (2,600 g)
    readings += [offset + int((412 + d) * cpg) for d in (0.2, -0.1, 0.0, 0.3, -0.2, 0.1)]
    put_down = None
    for i, raw in enumerate(readings):
        t = i / 10
        if put_down is None and f.to_grams(raw) > ITEM_ON_G:
            put_down = t                                          # the first raw reading above 20 g
        was_steady = f.steady
        g = f.add(raw)
        print(f"t={t:3.1f} s  raw={raw:>10,}  cleaned={g:7.1f} g  {'steady' if f.steady else ''}")
        if put_down is not None and f.steady and not was_steady:
            print(f"   -> steady weight {f.weight:.1f} g, {t - put_down:.1f} s after the item was put down")

kiosk/calibrate.py

Measuring the offset and counts per gram with a 500 g weight. 77 lines.

"""calibrate.py - measure the scale's zero and slope once, and save them in config.json.

Run on the Pi with the kiosk app stopped:
    systemctl --user stop kiosk.service
    python3 calibrate.py
    systemctl --user start kiosk.service
You need one known weight (500 g). The script asks for an empty tray, then for
the weight in the middle of the tray, and works out:
    offset          = median raw reading with the empty tray
    counts_per_gram = (median raw with the weight - offset) / 500
On a laptop, `python3 calibrate.py --simulate` walks through the same steps
with the simulated scale and no questions.
"""
import argparse
import json
from pathlib import Path
from statistics import median

import hx711

HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent
CONFIG = HERE / "config.json"
READINGS = 20                    # 20 readings = 2 s at 10 readings per second


def median_of_readings(scale, n=READINGS):
    """The median of n good readings; timed-out readings (None) are skipped."""
    values = [r for r in (scale.read_raw() for _ in range(n)) if r is not None]
    if len(values) < n // 2:
        raise SystemExit("The HX711 is not answering. Check VCC (pin 1), GND (pin 6), "
                         "DT (pin 29, GPIO 5) and SCK (pin 31, GPIO 6).")
    return median(values)


def ask(prompt, simulate):
    if simulate:
        print(prompt, "(simulated)")
    else:
        input(prompt)


def main():
    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser()
    ap.add_argument("--grams", type=float, default=500.0, help="the known check weight")
    ap.add_argument("--simulate", action="store_true", help="laptop walk-through with the simulated scale")
    args = ap.parse_args()
    scale = hx711.SimulatedHX711() if args.simulate else hx711.HX711()

    ask("Empty the tray and press Enter.", args.simulate)
    offset = median_of_readings(scale)
    print(f"empty tray: {offset:,.0f} counts")

    ask(f"Put the {args.grams:.0f} g weight in the middle of the tray and press Enter.", args.simulate)
    if args.simulate:
        scale.set_load(args.grams)
    loaded = median_of_readings(scale)
    print(f"with {args.grams:.0f} g: {loaded:,.0f} counts")

    cpg = (loaded - offset) / args.grams
    if cpg <= 0:
        raise SystemExit("The reading went DOWN when weight was added: swap the white and "
                         "green load cell wires at A- and A+ (chapter 4), then run this again.")
    print(f"counts_per_gram = ({loaded:,.0f} - {offset:,.0f}) / {args.grams:.0f} = {cpg:.1f}")

    config = json.loads(CONFIG.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))   # keep every other setting
    config.update({"offset": int(round(offset)), "counts_per_gram": round(cpg, 1),
                   "calibrated_with_grams": int(args.grams)})
    if args.simulate:
        print("simulated run: config.json not changed")
    else:
        CONFIG.write_text(json.dumps(config, indent=2) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
        print("saved in", CONFIG)
    scale.close()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

kiosk/camera.py

One fresh photo, and the item box from the blue-tray colour mask. 93 lines.

"""camera.py - take one fresh photo and find the item on the blue tray.

The camera is a Logitech C270 USB webcam, 640 x 480, 35 cm above the tray,
looking straight down. The tray is blue and no item is blue, so the item is
"everything that is not tray blue".
"""
import numpy as np

try:
    import cv2
except ImportError:          # lets the bill and filter tests run on a machine without OpenCV
    cv2 = None

WIDTH, HEIGHT = 640, 480
WARM_UP_FRAMES = 5           # thrown away after opening: exposure settles
FLUSH_FRAMES = 4             # buffered frames thrown away before each photo
MIN_ITEM_PIXELS = 400        # a smaller blob is a speck, not an item

# The tray in HSV colour space: hue 90..130 is blue (OpenCV hue runs 0..179).
TRAY_LOW = (90, 80, 50)
TRAY_HIGH = (130, 255, 255)


class Camera:
    def __init__(self, index=0):
        self.cap = cv2.VideoCapture(index)
        if not self.cap.isOpened():
            raise RuntimeError(f"camera {index} not found (is it plugged in? is /dev/video{index} there?)")
        self.cap.set(cv2.CAP_PROP_FRAME_WIDTH, WIDTH)
        self.cap.set(cv2.CAP_PROP_FRAME_HEIGHT, HEIGHT)
        for _ in range(WARM_UP_FRAMES):          # the first frames are often too dark
            self.cap.read()

    def take_photo(self):
        """One fresh BGR frame (480, 640, 3), or None if the camera failed."""
        for _ in range(FLUSH_FRAMES):            # drop frames that waited in the queue
            self.cap.grab()
        ok, frame = self.cap.read()              # this one is taken now
        return frame if ok else None

    def close(self):
        self.cap.release()


class FakeCamera:
    """For laptop demos: draws a blue tray with a coloured round 'item' on it."""

    def __init__(self, seed=0):
        self.rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
        self.colour = (40, 40, 200)             # BGR, set by the demo

    def take_photo(self):
        frame = np.zeros((HEIGHT, WIDTH, 3), np.uint8)
        frame[:] = (200, 120, 40)                # tray blue in BGR
        cx, cy = self.rng.integers(220, 420), self.rng.integers(160, 320)
        cv2.circle(frame, (int(cx), int(cy)), int(self.rng.integers(45, 80)), self.colour, -1)
        return frame

    def close(self):
        pass


def find_item_box(bgr):
    """The box (x, y, w, h) around the biggest non-blue blob, or None if the tray looks empty."""
    hsv = cv2.cvtColor(bgr, cv2.COLOR_BGR2HSV)
    tray = cv2.inRange(hsv, TRAY_LOW, TRAY_HIGH)            # 255 where the pixel is tray blue
    item = cv2.bitwise_not(tray)                            # 255 where it is NOT tray blue
    kernel = np.ones((5, 5), np.uint8)
    item = cv2.morphologyEx(item, cv2.MORPH_OPEN, kernel)   # remove specks smaller than 5 x 5
    contours, _ = cv2.findContours(item, cv2.RETR_EXTERNAL, cv2.CHAIN_APPROX_SIMPLE)
    if not contours:
        return None
    biggest = max(contours, key=cv2.contourArea)
    if cv2.contourArea(biggest) < MIN_ITEM_PIXELS:
        return None
    return cv2.boundingRect(biggest)                        # (x, y, w, h)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    # On the Pi: take one photo, find the box, save both. On a laptop: use the fake camera.
    try:
        cam = Camera(0)
    except Exception as e:
        print("no real camera:", e, "- using the fake camera")
        cam = FakeCamera()
    photo = cam.take_photo()
    box = find_item_box(photo)
    print("photo", photo.shape, "item box", box)
    if box:
        x, y, w, h = box
        cv2.rectangle(photo, (x, y), (x + w, y + h), (0, 165, 255), 2)
    cv2.imwrite("camera_test.jpg", photo)
    cam.close()

kiosk/bill.py

Prices, whole paise and the itemised bill. 99 lines.

"""bill.py - prices and the itemised bill, with money kept in whole paise.

Why paise: computers store decimal rupees like 8.78 only approximately, so
adding many of them can give 15.079999999999998 instead of 15.08. Whole
numbers of paise are exact, so every sum is exact. Rupees appear only when
the bill is printed.

>>> price_paise(412, 35)          # 412 g of tomato at Rs 35/kg
1442
>>> rupees(1442)
'Rs 14.42'
>>> price_paise(1033, 25)         # 2,582.5 paise rounds half up to 2,583
2583
>>> b = Bill()
>>> _ = b.add("Tomato", 412, 35)
>>> _ = b.add("Onion", 1032.6, 25)
>>> b.total_paise, rupees(b.total_paise)
(4025, 'Rs 40.25')
"""
import csv
from pathlib import Path


def load_prices(path):
    """Read prices.csv into a dictionary {"Tomato": 35, ...} of whole rupees per kg."""
    prices = {}
    with open(path, newline="", encoding="utf-8") as f:
        for row in csv.DictReader(f):
            prices[row["item"].strip()] = int(row["rupees_per_kg"])
    return prices


def check_prices(prices, class_names):
    """Stop at start-up if any class the network knows has no price."""
    missing = [name for name in class_names if name not in prices]
    if missing:
        raise ValueError(f"prices.csv has no price for: {', '.join(missing)}")


def price_paise(grams, rupees_per_kg):
    """Price of `grams` (rounded to whole grams) at `rupees_per_kg`, in whole paise.

    paise = grams x rupees_per_kg x 100 / 1000, rounded half up. All whole numbers.
    """
    g = int(round(grams))                     # the bill shows and charges whole grams
    return (g * rupees_per_kg * 100 + 500) // 1000


def money(paise):
    """1442 -> '14.42', using whole-number maths only."""
    return f"{paise // 100}.{paise % 100:02d}"


def rupees(paise):
    """1442 -> 'Rs 14.42'. Paise become rupees only when they are shown."""
    return "Rs " + money(paise)


class Bill:
    """The customer's bill: a list of lines and a total, all in paise."""

    def __init__(self):
        self.lines = []                       # each line: (name, grams, rupees_per_kg, paise)

    def add(self, name, grams, rupees_per_kg):
        line = (name, int(round(grams)), rupees_per_kg, price_paise(grams, rupees_per_kg))
        self.lines.append(line)
        return line

    def remove_last(self):
        if self.lines:
            self.lines.pop()

    def clear(self):
        self.lines = []

    @property
    def total_paise(self):
        return sum(line[3] for line in self.lines)

    def text(self):
        """The bill as lines of text, the same layout the screen shows."""
        rows = [f"{'Item':<14}{'Grams':>7}{'Rs/kg':>7}{'Rs':>9}"]
        for name, g, rpk, p in self.lines:
            rows.append(f"{name:<14}{g:>7}{rpk:>7}{money(p):>9}")
        rows.append("-" * 37)
        rows.append(f"{'Total':<28}{money(self.total_paise):>9}")
        return "\n".join(rows)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    import doctest
    print("doctest failures:", doctest.testmod().failed)
    prices = load_prices(Path(__file__).resolve().parent / "prices.csv")
    bill = Bill()
    for name, grams in [("Tomato", 412), ("Onion", 1032.6), ("Apple", 618), ("Lemon", 97)]:
        bill.add(name, grams, prices[name])
    print(bill.text())
    print("total:", rupees(bill.total_paise))

kiosk/kiosk_app.py

The touchscreen app: start-up, tick, states and screens. 286 lines.

"""kiosk_app.py - the touchscreen self-checkout app (Tkinter, 800 x 480, fullscreen).

Run on the Pi:        python3 kiosk_app.py
Run on a laptop demo: python3 kiosk_app.py --simulate --windowed
  (a simulated scale and a fake camera; keys 1-9, 0, Q, W put an item on the
   tray, the space bar takes it off)

Start-up order: load weights -> open camera -> set up GPIO -> read config.json
and prices.csv -> tare -> show the first screen -> start the tick.

The tick runs 10 times a second. Each time it reads the scale once, feeds the
filter, and moves between the five states:
  Waiting -> Settling -> Identifying -> Showing -> Remove -> Waiting
"Wrong item" (or a top chance below 0.60) opens the Choose screen; "Finish"
opens the Bill screen.
"""
import argparse
import json
import sys
import traceback
from pathlib import Path
import tkinter as tk

import numpy as np

HERE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent              # .../kiosk
CLASSIFIER_DIR = HERE.parent / "classifier"         # .../classifier
sys.path.insert(0, str(CLASSIFIER_DIR))             # so "import cnn_numpy" finds the shared layer code

from cnn_numpy import CLASSES, Net, crop_with_margin, prepare_image, top_k   # noqa: E402
from bill import Bill, check_prices, load_prices, price_paise, rupees       # noqa: E402
from weight_filter import WeightFilter                                       # noqa: E402
import camera                                                                # noqa: E402
import hx711                                                                 # noqa: E402

TICK_MS = 100              # the tick runs every 100 ms = 10 times a second
TARE_READINGS = 20         # 2 s of readings with the empty tray at start-up
NO_READING_LIMIT = 30      # 30 ticks (3 s) without a reading -> "Scale not responding"
CHANGED_G = 5.0            # a steady weight this far from the shown item means a new item

WAITING, SETTLING, IDENTIFYING, SHOWING, CHOOSING, REMOVE, BILL = (
    "waiting", "settling", "identifying", "showing", "choosing", "remove", "bill")

BG, TEXT, GREY = "#f4f7f8", "#1c2b33", "#5b7083"
TEAL, GREEN, ORANGE = "#0f7c8c", "#2f8f4e", "#d9822b"
BIG, MID, SMALL = ("DejaVu Sans", 40, "bold"), ("DejaVu Sans", 24), ("DejaVu Sans", 15)
BUTTON, CHOICE = ("DejaVu Sans", 20, "bold"), ("DejaVu Sans", 13, "bold")

# Laptop demo only: key -> (class name, grams, BGR colour for the fake camera).
DEMO_ITEMS = {"1": ("Apple", 618, (40, 30, 190)), "2": ("Beetroot", 260, (70, 20, 110)),
              "3": ("Brinjal", 340, (90, 30, 70)), "4": ("Brinjal White", 300, (210, 225, 230)),
              "5": ("Coconut", 540, (40, 70, 110)), "6": ("Guava", 210, (90, 200, 150)),
              "7": ("Lemon", 97, (60, 220, 230)), "8": ("Mosambi", 180, (70, 200, 170)),
              "9": ("Onion", 1032.6, (120, 110, 170)), "0": ("Pomegranate", 380, (50, 40, 170)),
              "q": ("Potato", 450, (110, 160, 200)), "w": ("Tomato", 412, (30, 40, 210))}


class KioskApp:
    def __init__(self, root, simulate=False, windowed=False):
        self.root = root
        self.simulate = simulate

        # 1. Load the weights once. The first forward pass is slow, so do a dummy one now.
        weights = CLASSIFIER_DIR / "weights.npz"
        if not weights.exists():
            sys.exit(f"{weights} not found. Train on the laptop, then copy it with scp.")
        self.net = Net()
        self.net.load_weights(weights)
        top_k(self.net, np.zeros((1, 3, 64, 64), np.float32))

        # 2. Open the camera (it throws away its first dark frames).
        self.camera = camera.FakeCamera() if simulate else camera.Camera(0)

        # 3. Set up the GPIO pins for the HX711 (the simulated scale is made in step 4).
        self.scale = None if simulate else hx711.HX711()

        # 4. Read the calibration and settings, and the price table.
        self.config = json.loads((HERE / "config.json").read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
        self.prices = load_prices(HERE / "prices.csv")
        check_prices(self.prices, CLASSES)
        self.threshold = float(self.config["confidence_threshold"])
        self.on_g = float(self.config["item_on_grams"])
        self.off_g = float(self.config["item_off_grams"])
        if simulate:
            self.scale = hx711.SimulatedHX711(self.config["offset"], self.config["counts_per_gram"])
        self.filter = WeightFilter(self.config["offset"], self.config["counts_per_gram"])

        # 5. Tare: the tray must be empty now.
        self.filter.tare([self.scale.read_raw() for _ in range(TARE_READINGS)])

        # 6. The window and the first screen, then 7. start the tick.
        self.bill = Bill()
        self.item = None             # the item being shown: {"grams": .., "guesses": [(index, chance), ..]}
        self.last_added = None       # the bill line just added, shown on the Remove screen
        self.note = ""               # a message for the status line, kept until the next item
        self.state = None
        self.no_reading = 0
        self.build_window(windowed)
        self.go(WAITING)
        self.root.after(TICK_MS, self.tick)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ window
    def build_window(self, windowed):
        r = self.root
        r.title("Self-checkout kiosk")
        r.configure(bg=BG)
        if windowed:
            r.geometry("800x480")
        else:
            r.attributes("-fullscreen", True)
            r.config(cursor="none")                  # a touchscreen needs no mouse pointer
        r.bind("<Control-q>", lambda e: self.quit())
        if self.simulate:
            r.bind("<Key>", self.demo_key)
        top = tk.Frame(r, bg=TEAL, height=48)
        top.pack(fill="x")
        tk.Label(top, text="Self-checkout", font=MID, fg="white", bg=TEAL).pack(side="left", padx=14)
        self.total_label = tk.Label(top, text="", font=MID, fg="white", bg=TEAL)
        self.total_label.pack(side="right", padx=14)
        self.body = tk.Frame(r, bg=BG)
        self.body.pack(fill="both", expand=True)
        self.status = tk.Label(r, text="", font=SMALL, fg=ORANGE, bg=BG)
        self.status.pack(fill="x", pady=4)

    def screen(self, big, small="", buttons=(), big_font=BIG, button_font=BUTTON):
        """Clear the middle of the window and draw one screen: a big line, a small line, buttons."""
        for w in self.body.winfo_children():
            w.destroy()
        self.big = tk.Label(self.body, text=big, font=big_font, fg=TEXT, bg=BG)
        self.big.pack(pady=(30, 6))
        self.small = tk.Label(self.body, text=small, font=MID, fg=GREY, bg=BG)
        if small:
            self.small.pack(pady=6)
        row = tk.Frame(self.body, bg=BG)
        row.pack(side="bottom", pady=18)
        for text, command, colour in buttons:
            tk.Button(row, text=text, command=command, font=button_font, fg="white", bg=colour,
                      activebackground=colour, activeforeground="white", relief="flat",
                      width=max(8, len(text) + 2), height=2).pack(side="left", padx=8)
        n = len(self.bill.lines)
        self.total_label.config(text=f"{n} item{'s' if n != 1 else ''}   {rupees(self.bill.total_paise)}")

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ states
    def go(self, state):
        """Change state and draw its screen."""
        self.state = state
        if state == WAITING:
            self.note = ""
            buttons = [("Finish", lambda: self.go(BILL), TEAL)] if self.bill.lines else []
            self.screen("Place an item", "One item, in the middle of the tray", buttons)
        elif state == SETTLING:
            self.screen("...", "Weighing, please wait")
        elif state == SHOWING:
            index, chance = self.item["guesses"][0]
            name = CLASSES[index]
            g, rpk = self.item["grams"], self.prices[name]
            self.screen(name, f"{g:.0f} g    Rs {rpk}/kg    {rupees(price_paise(g, rpk))}",
                        [("Add to bill", lambda: self.add(name), GREEN),
                         ("Wrong item", lambda: self.go(CHOOSING), ORANGE)])
        elif state == CHOOSING:
            self.choose_screen(full_list=not self.item["guesses"])
        elif state == REMOVE:
            if self.last_added:
                name, g, rpk, p = self.last_added
                self.screen("Take the item off", f"Added: {name}, {g} g, {rupees(p)}")
            else:
                self.screen("Take the item off", "Nothing was added")
        elif state == BILL:
            self.screen(self.bill.text(), "", [("Back", lambda: self.go(WAITING), GREY),
                                               ("New customer", self.new_customer, GREEN)],
                        big_font=("DejaVu Sans Mono", 18))

    def choose_screen(self, full_list=False):
        guesses = self.item["guesses"] or []
        sure = bool(guesses) and guesses[0][1] >= self.threshold
        title = "Which item is it?" if sure else "Not sure, please choose"
        if full_list:
            self.screen(title, "", [("Cancel", self.cancel, GREY)],
                        big_font=MID)
            grid = tk.Frame(self.body, bg=BG)
            grid.pack()
            for k, name in enumerate(CLASSES):
                tk.Button(grid, text=name, font=CHOICE, width=11, bg="white", fg=TEXT,
                          command=lambda n=name: self.add(n)).grid(row=k // 4, column=k % 4,
                                                                   padx=3, pady=3, ipady=9)
        else:
            buttons = [(CLASSES[i], lambda n=CLASSES[i]: self.add(n), TEAL) for i, _ in guesses[:3]]
            buttons.append(("Other items", lambda: self.choose_screen(full_list=True), GREY))
            self.screen(title, f"{self.item['grams']:.0f} g on the tray", buttons, big_font=MID,
                        button_font=CHOICE)             # smaller text: four buttons must fit in 800 pixels

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ the tick
    def tick(self):
        """Runs every 100 ms: one scale reading, one filter update, one state step."""
        try:
            raw = self.scale.read_raw(timeout=0) if self.scale.is_ready() else None
            self.no_reading = 0 if raw is not None else self.no_reading + 1
            grams = self.filter.add(raw)
            self.status.config(text="Scale not responding" if self.no_reading > NO_READING_LIMIT else self.note)
            self.step(grams)
        except Exception:
            traceback.print_exc()                    # keep the kiosk alive; the log shows what happened
        finally:
            self.root.after(TICK_MS, self.tick)

    def step(self, grams):
        s, f = self.state, self.filter
        if s == WAITING:
            if grams > self.on_g:
                self.go(SETTLING)
        elif s == SETTLING:
            self.big.config(text=f"{grams:.0f} g")   # live weight
            if grams < self.off_g:
                self.go(WAITING)                     # it was lifted off again
            elif f.steady and f.weight < self.on_g:
                self.go(WAITING)                     # steady but under 20 g: not an item
            elif f.steady:
                self.identify()
        elif s in (SHOWING, CHOOSING):
            if grams < self.off_g:
                self.go(WAITING)                     # taken off without adding: nothing is billed
            elif f.steady and abs(f.weight - self.item["grams"]) > CHANGED_G:
                self.go(SETTLING)                    # something was added or swapped: weigh again
        elif s == REMOVE:
            if grams < self.off_g:
                self.go(WAITING)
        # BILL: the scale is ignored while the customer reads the bill

    def identify(self):
        """One photo, one prediction. Called once per item, when the weight is steady."""
        self.state = IDENTIFYING
        guesses = None
        photo = self.camera.take_photo()
        box = camera.find_item_box(photo) if photo is not None else None
        if box is not None:
            x = prepare_image(crop_with_margin(photo, box))
            guesses = top_k(self.net, x, 3)
        else:
            self.note = "Could not see the item, please choose"
        self.item = {"grams": self.filter.weight, "guesses": guesses}
        if guesses and guesses[0][1] >= self.threshold:
            self.go(SHOWING)
        else:
            self.go(CHOOSING)

    # ------------------------------------------------------------------ buttons
    def add(self, name):
        self.last_added = self.bill.add(name, self.item["grams"], self.prices[name])
        self.go(REMOVE)

    def cancel(self):
        """Leave the Choose screen without billing anything."""
        self.last_added = None
        self.go(REMOVE)

    def new_customer(self):
        self.bill.clear()
        self.go(WAITING)

    def demo_key(self, event):
        key = event.char.lower()
        if key == " ":
            self.scale.set_load(0)
        elif key in DEMO_ITEMS:
            name, grams, colour = DEMO_ITEMS[key]
            self.camera.colour = colour
            self.scale.set_load(grams)

    def quit(self):
        self.scale.close()
        self.camera.close()
        self.root.destroy()


def main():
    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="Self-checkout kiosk app")
    parser.add_argument("--simulate", action="store_true", help="simulated scale and fake camera (laptop demo)")
    parser.add_argument("--windowed", action="store_true", help="an 800 x 480 window instead of fullscreen")
    args = parser.parse_args()
    root = tk.Tk()
    KioskApp(root, simulate=args.simulate, windowed=args.windowed)
    root.mainloop()


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

How to run it

On the laptop: check, prepare, train

cd classifier
python cnn_numpy.py
python gradient_check.py
python train.py --synthetic
python prepare_dataset.py --images ../dataset/images --labels ../dataset/labels
python train.py

cnn_numpy.py checks the fast convolution against the slow one and counts the weights. This is its real output:

fast conv equals slow conv: True
weights in the network: 80604
output shape for 2 pictures: (2, 12)

gradient_check.py prints the worst relative difference for each of the 10 weight arrays (shown in chapter 9) and ends with:

weights re-measured with a smaller nudge (kinks): 5 of 200
biggest relative difference: 4.9e-07
PASS

train.py --synthetic trains the real network for 8 epochs on 720 drawn shapes in 12 classes and tests it on 180 more. It takes about 30 seconds. This is my real run:

crops: train 720, validation 180, test 180; chance level 8.3%
epoch  1  loss 2.535  train  18.5%  val  21.1%  (3 s)  <- best so far
epoch  2  loss 1.667  train  54.4%  val  57.2%  (3 s)  <- best so far
epoch  3  loss 1.128  train  62.1%  val  64.4%  (3 s)  <- best so far
epoch  4  loss 0.886  train  63.5%  val  63.3%  (3 s)
epoch  5  loss 0.779  train  66.7%  val  68.3%  (3 s)  <- best so far
epoch  6  loss 0.698  train  82.1%  val  84.4%  (3 s)  <- best so far
epoch  7  loss 0.562  train  90.3%  val  90.0%  (3 s)  <- best so far
epoch  8  loss 0.512  train  85.0%  val  84.4%  (3 s)
best epoch 7: train 90.3%, validation 90.0%
test accuracy 87.8% (158 of 180)
wrote classifier/synthetic_run/weights.npz and classifier/synthetic_run/metrics.json

87.8% against a chance level of 8.3% proves that the whole chain learns: data, augmentation, forward, loss, backward, momentum, best-epoch selection, testing and saving. The accuracy climbs from 18.5% in epoch 1 to 90.3% in epoch 7. Epoch 8 dropped back a little, and the best-epoch rule correctly kept epoch 7's weights.

Be careful

The synthetic shapes are a test of the code, not of fruit recognition. They prove the network learns; they say nothing about mosambis. The fruit numbers in this book come from the real run on the version 1 photos.

prepare_dataset.py prints how many photos it kept, how many boxes of Capsicum and Chilly it dropped, and a table of crops per class and pile. With my photos it ended like this (predicted values, as in chapter 10):

photos kept: 604   boxes dropped: {'Capsicum': 41, 'Chilly': 66}
class           train   val  test  total
...
total            1290   277   283   1850
wrote classifier/data/dataset_64.npz and classifier/data/split.csv
Measured value (predicted, replace with your own number after your test)

The full run of train.py printed one line per epoch, about 45 s each, and ended with: "best epoch 34: train 98.1%, validation 93.5%" and "test accuracy 92.6% (262 of 283)". metrics.json held the confusion matrix of chapter 10 and the 0.60 rule counts of chapter 11 (266 sure with 256 right, 17 unsure with 6 right).

On the Pi: test the parts, calibrate, run

cd ~/Self-Checkout-Kiosk/kiosk
python3 hx711.py
python3 camera.py
python3 calibrate.py
python3 kiosk_app.py

hx711.py prints 20 raw readings. With an empty tray they should all be close to the offset (about 123,450), and pressing the tray gently should make them rise. camera.py saves camera_test.jpg with the item box drawn in orange. calibrate.py asks for an empty tray and then the 500 g weight. Its real output with the simulated scale (on the Pi the numbers are the real ones):

Empty the tray and press Enter. (simulated)
empty tray: 123,418 counts
Put the 500 g weight in the middle of the tray and press Enter. (simulated)
with 500 g: 338,922 counts
counts_per_gram = (338,922 - 123,418) / 500 = 431.0
simulated run: config.json not changed

kiosk_app.py opens the fullscreen app. Ctrl+Q closes it; in normal use systemd starts it at boot (chapter 5).

python3 kiosk_app.py --simulate --windowed
